# Retro draft for #1810

## 1. Field feedback

<!-- field_feedback: The person's answer, asked before anything is read: what came back from real use since the last retro, what went well, what cost them time, what they want changed; quoted. -->

The person, asked by the SE session that started this run: no field feedback beyond what is already in issues. That is this issue's comment of 24 Nov, quoted:

> This is an operation we're going to do often.

> you're not auditing; you're just merging.

> The agent needs to have a reset button where it just restarts, sends the initial prompt again, and cleans everything up.

The person's requirement for this run, verbatim:

> doing one retro needs to clear the retro debt overall.

Further feedback from the person during this run, verbatim:

> also, for the retro: i feel like we should move the scripts to a 'agent tools' component in go with subcomponents for each, ideally presented via mcp. the retro has a step that identifies scripts that need to be integrated, yes? In general, we have had this in the past: scripts and tools that take a long time: can they run parallel, and they always need to give status updates

With it, the person asked to treat the Go agent-tools component as a proposal to weigh against SE's and kern's existing core (reuse before duplicate), to judge bounded parallelism, ordered writes and progress (phase, done/total where known, elapsed, stalls and failures, through the CLI and MCP where the host has it) as separate requirements, to check whether earlier fixes of the same took effect, and to read repeated script runs as evidence for integration, not as proof that Go or a new component is needed.

The SE session also handed over three observations from 24 Nov: a routine sync of a mirror from the public line took about 20 minutes because it was run as an audit, not a merge; the SE session worked around the open-retro filing gate with a raw `gh issue create` (#1811, since closed); and it ran this retro inline instead of starting a retro-agent session, saying it could not start sessions although its host offers `create_session`. A second retro issue, filed by hand outside `retro.py` on 5 Nov on another tracker of the same tree, had stayed open since; this run covers it.


## 2. Collect

<!-- collect: Per project, what retro.py collect read: what came back, refusals, escalations, suite times, session costs, scripts run three or more times, planning misses, the field reports' answers to the standing questions, and the last retro's classes measured again, rate before against rate now. -->

Judge's notes on what collect read:

- **The previous retro's findings were never filed.** Every one of retro #864's 12 findings shows `issues: none`: no work issue names `retro #864 finding <k>`, so the scoring above reads nothing. Measured against the tree by hand instead: 1 (runner variables, `tests/conftest.py`), 4 (docs build in the focused tests, AGENTS.md), 5 (intake and draft take files; the relay replaced it), 7 (discovery packages, the work model's Local tickets), 9 (usage-limit restart, `limit_restart.py`), 10 (steps only the person approves), 11 (`collect --since`) and 12 (273 metrics runs read from landings this window) are fixed; 2 (unquieted `git init`) is past, since every test now reaches git's fake door; 6 (Without gh) is past, the relay owns it; 3 (the guard and the generated reference) is not verified; 8 (workers do not stop) holds, with 2 of 251 closed issues without a field report (#1376, #1474).
- **The rule check `work-issue`** counts 5 landings without a work issue; all five are release landings (#1729, #1783, #1791, #1798, #1807), which name none by design: noise, not breaks.
- **The hand-filed retro of 5 Nov** proposed four fixes; against the tree: `move --to done` refuses unchecked acceptance items (fixed, `checklist_and_evidence_errors`); comments that arrive after a worker last read them are refused by `field-report --check` (fixed); what a worker may call a blocker is the box (past); acceptance items needing a throw-away issue are superseded, as no command files issues (wrk-007).
- **Collect itself** ran about 20 minutes with its GitHub reads one after another and no progress line beyond each call's name: one live instance of the person's feedback.
- **Size.** The draft is about 346 KB, over hygiene's 200 KB limit for a tracked file; retro #864's doc was 123 KB.

### The field reports' answers to the standing questions

#### What came back from real use since the last retro?

- #1678: The real retro #864 doc reads back as a filed retro; its classes carry no cause or home, so the next collect measures them (rate before 0.03) and leaves them open.
- #1751: The `check` job refused the adopted branch twice: hygiene on a tracked binary (`Microsoft/Windows/PowerShell/ModuleAnalysisCache`) and on a README reference to the removed `.github/agents/`; the docs build refused two AGENTS.md paragraphs as over five lines. `deliver` pushed but stopped at the pull request, as gh here has no account. After delivery, main gained `642c8ae` and `a1caf5a`, each of which still updated the removed `.github/` copies and conflicted.
- #1752: The real `git worktree add` layout: `local_list_path` on the linked worktree printed the main checkout's `.public-check.local`. The integrator's message that main (a1caf5a, #1678) changed the Retro form, which this report follows after merging main again.
- #1757: The guard ran for real on this checkout: it listed `components/forms` (one `.pyc` from the restored snapshot), removed it and passed. In this shallow clone (51 commits) `kern`'s deletion is outside the history, so `kern/python/kern/__pycache__` is not seen as a leftover at all here; the integrator's deeper clone did see it.
- #1758: This session's restart on the installed se 0.14.0: `instance github has no port template-accept` (Spec gaps). Go itself: `go: errors parsing go.mod: require kern: version "v2.1.0" invalid: should be v0 or v1, not v2` on the first build at the pin, before any code.
- #1773: Landing PR #1772's `check` failed 12 builds on a fresh runner: obs-html 0.5.0 needs obs-html-style-generic, which obs 10.0's install did not bring. A real install with obs 10.3.2 on an empty folder installed both.
- #1786: The docs workflow's Timeline step failing on obs#274's Goal (the work issue's Situation); reproduced here with the real obs build before the change.
- #1790: - The run of the guide on a project at 5.134 sent back three faults, each fixed in 172a0208: - `sync` refused the obs pin at SE 8; - the template load check assumed a `README.md`; - nothing could find se as a component.

#### Which scripts did the AI write that a machine could own?

- #1678: Three scratch generators wrote the question notes and the template examples from Python lists; once written, the notes are the owners, so nothing remains to own.
- #1751: Two inline Python snippets: one listed the sentences the landing guard wants verbatim (`deleted_authored_places`) and pasted them into the report; one rewrote report fields after a merge. `work_tickets.py field-report --draft` could own the first: write the guard's sentence list into "Removed behaviour" as a skeleton for the worker to annotate.
- #1752: The python heredocs that filled the report's evidence rows and ticket evidence; `work_tickets.py move --to done` could take `--evidence <text>` as `create` takes `--acceptance`.
- #1757: A small inline Python script that writes evidence into tickets and checks their acceptance items; `work_tickets.py move --to done` could take `--evidence <text>`.
- #1773: A short Python edit of components.json to remove the style, run twice to reproduce the partial case; a fake listing in the tests now owns that case.
- #1786: Three probe scripts in the scratchpad that built a hand-edited Timeline note through `timeline.build` to see what obs accepts. A command that builds one note's Markdown through obs and prints its refusals (obs's own `obs-document` on a one-note vault) would own it.
- #1790: - `checks.sh` and `steps.sh` in the scratchpad: the guide's checks and steps, written out. `upgrade_guide.py` could own a `check` verb that runs a section's **Check:** commands in a project, so the boot runs them, not an agent.

#### Anything worth porting to other projects, or so bad we remove it?

- #1678: The `own(name)` and `sections(template, level)` helpers in `scripts/templates.py` let any SE document nest a block without copying its text; other documents with repeated question lists could use them.
- #1758: Remove: SE's Python render of kern's template JSON (`kern_template`, `kern_fields`) and its agreement test, done here. Port: the filled-template hand-off to any application that writes through kern's GitHub door.
- #1790: - The guide's shape and the release refusal, to obs, obs-html and kern (tickets above).

#### Has the process gone stale?

- #1790: - `work_tickets.py refresh` trusts a relay snapshot up to 10 minutes old, so a comment posted just after the snapshot goes unseen (Spec gaps); a refresh after a peer's notice could read afresh.

#### What would have stopped this from being produced at all?

- #1678: A check that every template nesting a block adapter has a Go port of its checker, at template load, would have refused the overhaul-judgment node before its cases were generated.
- #1751: The binary check of `scripts/hygiene.py` in the pre-commit hook would have refused the PowerShell cache before the first commit; today hygiene runs only in the `check` job and by hand.
- #1752: The submodule fallback in the original change: a test of a `.git` file without `commondir` beside the worktree one would have shown it before the pull request was opened.
- #1757: The guard's own test fixture (`.venv`, `__pycache__` in a deleted folder) already held a `.pyc` in `adapters/html/src`; a case for a folder with only cache would have shown at SE#1662 that the remedy is a destructive clean of harmless files.
- #1758: A check in kern's release that an application builds at the new major (kern's `template/` built at `v<major>`) would have caught the module-path problem before SE's move; ticketed above.
- #1773: A check that SE's obs range admits the obs release its newest required adapter needs: obs-html 0.5.0 required an obs install feature SE's `^10.0` lock (10.0.0) lacked.
- #1786: A Timeline build test whose snapshot quotes link syntax, as this branch adds, would have caught it when `goal()` began deleting backticks.
- #1790: - A release check that refused a major without an upgrade section from the first major on: the knowledge would have been written at each landing, not reconstructed from 13 pull requests.

#### What did the shell do that a command could have done? Name it.

- #1678: `git clean -fdX components/forms kern`, which `work_tickets.py suite` names as its remedy for a cached container's leftover bytecode; `bootstrap.sh` could clean folders whose tracked files are gone at start.
- #1751: Without gh, `deliver` ends after its push; the worker then runs `pr-body` with `--issue-body`, `--issue-url` and `--field-report` and posts the output with `update_pull_request` by hand. `deliver` could print that tool call with its input for the session to run through the relay, as `integrate` does for its GitHub steps.
- #1752: Extracting the guard's verbatim authored paragraph from its refusal into the report: `field-report --draft` could write the rewritten paragraphs the guard will ask for under "Removed behaviour" itself.
- #1757: `obs-components install obs-html-style-generic` had to be run by hand before `tests/test_docs.py` could pass: the session start could install the docs style the docs build needs.
- #1758: Splitting one file's two hunks into two commits took a copy-and-restore of `wiring.toml` in the shell; `git add -p` is interactive and refused here.
- #1773: `work_tickets.py intake` failed with `No module named 'kern'` until `se_modules.py sync kern` placed kern's SDK; intake could sync kern itself first.
- #1786: `gh issue view` is refused here (GraphQL); `work_tickets.py intake` answered through kern's relay after `issue_read`, as designed.
- #1790: - Building a project at an old SE (`git archive v5.134.2`, a hand-written `se.lock`, `se_workflows.py write` of the old SE). A `se_new.py --at <version>` fixture would make it one command.

#### Did anything local earn promotion upstream?

- #1758: `se_modules.go_version` (Go's spelling of a require above major 1) belongs in kern's release docs; ticketed above.
- #1790: - `upgrade_guide.py` itself, to kern's tool belt once the components keep guides of the same shape.

#### For each failed check, what rule would have caught it?

- #1678: The docs build refused a timestamp in the retro template's example (obs's provenance check) and four long README paragraphs; the shared-case size limit refused the overhaul's case file at 210 KB. Each is already a rule; running `tests/test_docs.py` and `tests/template_cases.py` with the first template change would have caught them earlier.
- #1751: - hygiene `binary`: the same check in pre-commit (above). - hygiene `references`: `se_plugin.py check` could run the references check over the files the build reads, so a commit that removes a layout fails where it removes it. - docs build, long paragraphs: `tests/test_docs.py` in the focused tests AGENTS.md names for a change to AGENTS.md; the other checkout did not run it.
- #1752: `work_tickets.py sync` failed on `se.lock` modified by the session start (its new generated header): the start's module sync should leave a checked-out work branch's tracked files alone. `field-report --check` refused rows named "1." instead of "Requirement 1" and the rewritten AGENTS.md paragraph not quoted: the template's Delivered comment could name the row form.
- #1757: `tests/test_docs.py` failed 3 tests at base and with the change: the obs style `obs-html-style-generic` was not installed in `/root/.config/obs`; after installing it, 22 passed. A start check that the docs build's components are installed would have caught it. `work_tickets.py intake` first failed with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'kern'` until `se_modules.py sync kern` placed kern.
- #1758: The PR's `release / build` failed in `kern-at-pin`, whose own regex read go.mod's require beside `se_modules`' (two readers of one pin; a rule "one reader per fact", gen-002, would have caught it). `tests/test_runme.py` failed only in the complete suite (kern's RUNME copy gained a marker); a focused rule "a moved kern pin runs every test that reads `.se/modules/kern`" would have caught it in the focused step. `go build` at the pin (module path, see above) and `go vet` (`tmpl.Check undefined` in `kern_test.go`); the rule "build first at a new pin" caught both before any change.
- #1773: `work_tickets.py intake`: ModuleNotFoundError for kern; the rule that a command needing kern's SDK syncs kern at its pin first would have caught it.
- #1786: My first run of the new tests failed on two expected strings that forgot `text()` escapes the comma; the rule is the existing one (write the expectation from `text()`'s escape set).
- #1790: - `test_template_cases` and the Go resolver test failed after the refusal text moved from se 6 to se 8: the Python and Go engines each hold the text. One owner of the text, shared by both engines, would have changed it in one place.

#### What goes well, and what makes it go well?

- #1678: The template engine's nesting: a block note with 13 fields gave the check, the draft skeleton and the refusal text for all three documents at once.
- #1751: Each refusal named its file and line and the fix (`git rm --cached`, the paragraph's line), so each fix took one edit. `field-report --check` named every missing sentence verbatim.
- #1752: The adopted branch merged main twice without conflict, and every local gate passed at its first run, because the change is one function with a direct test.
- #1757: The guard was one small function with fakeable git, so the change and its tests stayed in process and fast (0.76 s).
- #1758: Building at the new pin first: it showed all three breaks (go.mod spelling, the removed form check, the tool list) in one run, and the Go template component's `fill`/`accept` already existed, so the change was wiring.
- #1773: obs already owned required components (`requirements`, `installed`), so SE only reads them: a small change.
- #1786: Probing the real obs build first showed that backslash escapes do not stop a wikilink, which settled the escape before any code.
- #1790: - Two research agents in parallel, one for SE's majors and one for the components, each writing notes with a source line per change. They made the guide checkable against the code.

#### What goes badly, each with its moment in the log?

- #1678: The overhaul's judged-block node passed the Python suite and failed only in `go test ./...` (`137 of 2788 answers differ`), after its commit `2a1ba74`.
- #1751: - The first `deliver` ended in `work_tickets.py deliver needs the GitHub CLI (gh), which is installed but not signed in`, after its push. - `pr-body` dropped the HTML comments the report quoted (the `copilot` marker comment became an empty code span); found by reading the body before posting. - Two merges of main (`5bb5981`, `c659919`) conflicted on generated `.github/` files that main's integrations still rewrote. - `work_tickets.py push --redeliver` after the second merge was refused: another session had pushed `c659919`, the same merge, at 11:55; this worker dropped its identical unpushed merge.
- #1752: `work_tickets.py deliver` (plugin se) pushed, then stopped: "needs the GitHub CLI (gh), which is installed but not signed in", although `se_relay.py next` answered "kern's GitHub relay waits for no GitHub tool call"; the description was updated by hand with `pr-body` and `update_pull_request`. It did not block. The cloud host's Stop hook asked to re-author the commits to another identity; not followed, as the worker definition says.
- #1757: Intake failed on the missing kern module (`se_relay.py` line 189) and the docs tests on the missing style: both container setup, not the change.
- #1758: Intake failed first with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'kern'` until `se_modules.py sync kern` placed kern's clone; a cloud start had not placed it.
- #1773: intake's first run: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'kern'` from scripts/se_relay.py line 189.
- #1786: The installed SE (5.134.2) drafted an older field-report template without the standing questions; redrafted with the checkout's `scripts/work_tickets.py` (12.0.0).
- #1790: - A research note filed PR 1785 under an unreleased SE 13. Release v12.0.1 (`bbeb6df5`) shows it shipped as a patch, and 7ec9322d moved it.

#### What do the thoughts say that the actions do not?

- #1790: - None

#### Start, stop, keep, more, less.

- #1678: Start: run `go test ./...` in `components` with the first change to `templates/`. Keep: one block per question set. Less: hand-written examples in template notes.
- #1751: - Start: running hygiene in pre-commit. - Stop: integrations regenerating a layout a delivered pull request removes. - Keep: verbatim refusals naming file and line. - More: `deliver` printing its remaining GitHub steps without gh. - Less: hand-copying the guard's sentence list.
- #1752: Start: a submodule case in tests of any `.git`-file reader. Stop: none. Keep: the real-layout check outside the suite. More: none. Less: none.
- #1757: Start: install kern and the docs style at session start. Stop: None. Keep: guards that name their remedy. More: in-process tests on real files under `tmp_path`. Less: remedies that need a destructive shell command.
- #1758: Start: a kern release check that builds an application at the new major. Stop: None. Keep: build first at a moved pin. More: in-process Go tests over SE's real wiring.toml. Less: Python mirrors of kern's internals.
- #1773: Start: syncing kern before intake. Stop: tests that run installs as processes. Keep: real runs against an empty machine folder. More: fakes of obs's listing. Less: None.
- #1786: Start: give Timeline build tests issue text that quotes Markdown and link syntax. Stop: None. Keep: probing obs before choosing an escape. More: None. Less: None.
- #1790: - Start: run each guide's steps on a project at the old version before delivering. - Stop: trusting a research note's version mapping without the release commit. - Keep: one test that every major has its section. - More: mechanical checks that name their file. - Less: checks that pass on any input.

#### Which rule, prompt or tool made each bad output, and what change stops it?

- #1678: The worker agent's focused-test list names the docs build for published Markdown but not the Go components suite for `templates/`; `AGENTS.md` "Tests" could name `components` `go test` as focused for a change under `templates/`.
- #1751: - The stray binary: a test run in the other checkout wrote PowerShell's cache into the tree. Change: `.githooks/pre-commit` runs `scripts/hygiene.py`'s binary check on the staged files. - The empty code span: `work_tickets.py pr-body` strips HTML comments, including ones inside code spans. Change: `pr-body` keeps comments inside backticks, in `scripts/work_tickets.py`.
- #1752: The submodule's superproject list: the original `local_list_path`'s fallback to the git directory's grandparent; fixed in `scripts/public_check.py` (2edb2f1), only a git directory with `commondir` reads the main checkout's list.
- #1757: The refusal of SE#1662 named `git clean -fdX` for harmless cache; the guard (`scripts/work_tickets.py` `refuse_leftover_folders`) now removes it itself.
- #1773: The 12 CI failures: `se.lock` held obs 10.0.0 while obs-html 0.5.0 needed obs 10.3.2's install; the pin move here fixes it (se.toml, se.lock).
- #1786: The broken build: `goal()` in `scripts/timeline.py` deleted backticks to make plain text, and `text()` trusted backslashes against wikilinks; fixed in this branch (`spans()`, `quoted_links()`).
- #1790: - The template load check passed on a TOML-only `templates/`, because it reads no TOML. The guide pairs it with `ls templates/*.toml`. `scripts/templates.py check --tree` could refuse a `templates/*.toml` other than `terms.toml`.

- #981: **A flaky host test, from before this branch:** `TestAClientStopsTheCore` (`kern/host/host_test.go`) fails about once in 100 to 500 runs with `call.core.stop: nats: connection closed`, at `729eaed` as on this branch (`go test -count=500 -run TestAClientStopsTheCore ./host/`: 1, 1 and 3 failures at base, 3, 2 and 1 here). The core answers the stop, then shuts the bus down before the server has delivered the answer to the client. Remedy: on stop, wait until the asker has had the answer, for example by closing the bus only after the client's connection is gone or after a short drain, then hold the test at `-count=1000`.

Here the lines arrive with the final answer, since a component's reply on `cmd.<i>.>` is one message (`Response: MaxMsgs 1`). Next: a progress subject the instance may publish on, which the core forwards to the caller's inbox.

Each output names how many issues it read, and readers take it only above 0. Next step, one work issue for kern's GitHub component: list reads (open issues, closed `work` issues, open pull requests to a base) and issue comments as snapshot families, then the fleet component reads `starting` from them.

- #1036: **Issue-worthy items for the dispatcher (I file no issues, per the owner's rule):**
- #1036: the environment: fresh cloud sessions install SE 5.0.0 from `/root/.claude/skills/se`, whose hooks post nothing to kern; until a current SE loads there, the relay and every one-call path stay unreachable live;
- #1036: once that holds, the real-landing check above;
- #1036: `Stepped` runs a streamed command in a thread with stdout and stderr captured process-wide across its steps, so another command of the same component while it waits writes into its capture; a component process that runs calls side by side (PR #1019's "Not delivered") would end that.
- #1047: No requirement or acceptance item is unmet. One procedure step was skipped: the suite was not timed before the change on `fa0d4b0`; the last measurement on main is #990's 43.5 s at `bae0819`, against 41.9 s now. `work_tickets.py suite --compare origin/main` would give the interleaved verdict.
- #1053: **Issue-worthy, for the dispatcher (I file no issues):**
- #1053: kern tells a component nothing when it abandons a call (a request failed, the core ended), so a streamed command's thread (`Stepped`) waiting on a step lives until `STEP_ANSWER_WAIT` (65 min). The old rule that a new call of the command gives that thread up would kill a call running beside it, so it is gone (Removed behaviour). Such a thread now holds no directory while it waits. A `cmd.<i>.<command>` stop word from the core when `CallSaying` ends a call that still has a continuation would end it at once.
- #1053: The process has one working directory. A call in another directory waits until the calls in the current one end. An interactive routed call waits at most `CALL_WAIT` (60 s) and then runs directly. A call kept waiting longer is not starved forever, but nothing orders the waiters.
- #1053: `kern/README.md`'s `cmd.<i>.<command>` row still says a step answers `lines`, which PR #1048 removed; out of this issue's scope, left as is.
- #1053: `gofmt -l` flags `kern/components/github/github_test.go` at main; not touched here.
- #1058: **Issue-worthy, for the dispatcher (I file no issues):**
- #1058: A caller killed outright (its session ends, the core lives on for another session) sends no `call.core.abandon`; its call runs until it ends or the core ends. "The session ending" is covered where the core ends: the core gives every call up (`ErrCoreEnds`), and its bus closing ends the component processes. A per-caller liveness check would need the bus's system events.
- #1058: A request already out runs on when its call is given up (`index.serve` drops its answer): the github component's `step` verb keeps its outbox entry until its step-wait (60 min) or an answer, so the session may still be pointed at a step nobody waits for. A `Serve` that takes a context would drop it at once.
- #1058: A script's main already running when its call is given up runs to its end (Python cannot stop a thread); only waits (a step's answer, the directory line) end at once.
- #1061: **Issue-worthy, for the dispatcher (I file no issues):**
- #1061: A declared (Python) process's verb is not told when the call that asked it is given up: the core stops waiting (`RequestWithContext`) and drops the answer, but the process serves on. A stop word on `io.<i>.<verb>`, like the one on `cmd.<i>.<command>`, would end it.
- #1061: `kern/kerntest`, the Go fake core, gives no call up (it answers no `call.core.abandon` and has no dead-caller check); only the real core does.
- #1064: **A fresh cloud session on main, quoted.** It needs `se-v0.2.0` published, which only the landing's push to main does. Next step after the landing: check that the `se-release` and `kern-release` runs on the landed commit end with `kern-release: published se-v0.2.0` / `kern-v0.4.0` (nothing to do by hand; where GitHub refuses the tag, allow it in the tag ruleset and rerun the workflow on main), then start a fresh cloud session and quote `se status` (`~/.se/bin/se-0.2.0 status --root <checkout>`). A session that starts before the release is out builds `se` from `components/` in the background (bootstrap.sh's fallback, about 20 s cold) and the greeting says so.
- #1065: **The live half of the acceptance** (a test repository, a linked `check` under 30 s): I cannot create repositories, so nothing ran live. Steps for the owner or dispatcher, on a private test repository `<owner>/<test>`: 1. Add `se.toml` with `[project] identity = "<identity>"` and `[tests] command = ["sh", "-c", "sleep 90"]`, `trust_integrator = true`, `full_every = 3`. 2. Add `.github/workflows/check.yml` as README "Trusted landings" shows (jobs `trust` and `check`, `on: pull_request` plus `schedule`, permissions `contents: read`, `checks: write`, `actions: read`), with `uses: mb-89/SE/.github/actions/integrator-pass@<the se commit this lands as>`. 3. Add `.github/workflows/se-pass-record.yml` as SE's, its step `uses: mb-89/SE/.github/actions/integrator-pass@<same ref>` with `mode: record`; push both to main. 4. Open a work pull request, then `work_tickets.py integrate <n>` from the integrator's account: the plan dispatches the record, then opens the landing pull request. Link its `check` run: the `trust` job's notice names the record, and the run's duration must be under 30 s (the 90 s suite does not run). 5. Push a commit to main by hand before the next landing's check starts (or reopen an old landing pull request): its `check` must run the 90 s suite ("is of tree …"). 6. The third landing (`full_every = 3`) must run the suite; the Actions tab lists the nightly `schedule` run of `check`.
- #1065: **kern-battery.yml**, the reusable workflow kern applications call, does not use the trust yet: it would call the action at a kern release tag that does not carry it yet. Next step: once a kern tag carries `.github/actions/integrator-pass`, add the `trust` job to `kern-battery.yml` as in README "Trusted landings" (for the dispatcher to file).
- #1065: The record's run costs a started minute on a private repository, like a trusted `check`: the saving is the full suite's minutes less those two. A record without its own workflow run needs a tool that writes a commit status or check run, which the GitHub MCP tools lack.
- #1072: Requirement 7 is unit-tested; GitHub's own queue, cancel and re-run of `check-rerun.yml` run for the first time on this pull request's CI and the next queued leg. Whether a re-run by the Actions token is allowed is the one part no fake shows.
- #1072: **Issue-worthy, for the dispatcher (I file no issues):**
- #1072: The plugin clone in this session is at e5ed9eb (5.47.4), older than main f43fdb3, and did not refresh at start. That is PR #1069's predicted gap: a cached environment's clone predates the self-refresh, and moves only once the environment is rebuilt. Item 1's fix reaches agents only once their plugin carries it.
- #1072: The session start raced itself: `core.log` held `se: serving ..., pid 255` and then `kern serve: a core already runs ..., pid 255`, and by my first command no core ran and no standing file stood.
- #1072: Until `se-v0.2.1` and `kern-v0.4.1` are cut by the landing, a session whose checkout carries this wiring and fetches the released `se` 0.2.0 refuses to start (`instance fleet has no port issue-lists`). Bootstrap builds from `components/` where no release matches the pin, so this holds only between the landing and the cut.
- #1081: The acceptance's "contract suite passes 200 of 200 on Windows in CI" is narrowed to the race's own cases: the three derived-value cases and the late subscriber, 200 runs on Windows and macOS (run 43503963150). The whole real leg takes about 8 s a round on Linux, so 200 rounds would not fit the leg's 45 minutes. The step ran once and was dropped again; whether CI should repeat those cases permanently, as it does `TestAHookWithNoCoreFailsOpenAtOnce`, is the integrator's call (about a minute a leg).
- #1081: **Issue-worthy, for the dispatcher (I file no issues):**
- #1081: After a restored boot, the first hook still reports the earlier boot's core as "the core ended without its end" with that boot's last log lines (this session's PostToolUse said so). That is true of the earlier boot but noise for the new session. Telling the two apart needs the boot identity (a boot id or boot time per platform) in the standing file.
- #1081: `core.log` is appended across boots with no timestamps, so a session cannot tell its own lines from an earlier boot's; #1072's "serving, then already runs" read as one start. Proposal: `serve` starts each run with a line naming its start time.
- #1081: This session's plugin is at 5.47.4 and its released `se` 0.2.0 refused this checkout's wiring (`instance fleet has no port issue-lists`) until I started the branch's own `se` by hand: the gap #1079's report foresaw until `se-v0.2.1` reaches a session's cache.

- #1117: **Requirement 1 and Acceptance 1, the one-off's cause:** I could not reproduce "step 4: no answer, line or beat within 1m0s". Every faithful reproduction lands at base (above), so the acceptance's "fails at today's base" is not met by the end-to-end test. What would produce the one-off's symptoms and is now closed: the step's answer failing to go out after the beat stopped (runner fix), and a pause over one minute (StepWait now ten minutes). Not closed, untested here: the component's bus connection dropping mid-call (the Python peer has no reconnect, and its beat thread exits silently on a send error). Next step: the next relay landing keeps `.se/.runtime/core.log`, where the runner prints a step thread's traceback, and, on a stall, runs `se_relay.py next` and `se status`, so its field report says which.
- #1117: **Requirement 5, "with no tool calls":** the guards run after one read, the pull request, which names the branch and carries the report the field-report guard checks; before it neither is known. A merge conflict or an unnamed deletion now fails with that one tool call, before the work issue's reads and every plan step.

The 2026-07-28 spec's `server/discover` needs SDK v1.7.0 or later, which needs Go 1.25, while `kern/go.mod`, `components/go.mod` and `kern/template/go.mod` pin Go 1.24.7.

Untested there: Copilot (VS Code and CLI) spawning `se mcp --root <checkout>` from its `mcp.json` and listing the five tools; its approval of a tool by `readOnlyHint` and by `--allow-tool='se(<tool>)'`; a call answered `starting`, then retried; a paged answer read page by page; the lease returned when Copilot ends the server (EOF or SIGTERM); and Copilot's tool-call timeout against `fleet-check`. Claude Code's spawn by `mcp.json` is untested too: no `.mcp.json` is committed (correction 6, #1123's).
- #1122: **Lead (d), a handle for long commands:** not made; it is more than a small change. Next step for the dispatcher: a work issue for the door to answer a call that outlives a wait the caller sets (default below the host's tool timeout) with a handle, `{"handle": <id>, "state": "running"}`, kept while the core runs the call, and a `wait`/`result` argument (or tool) that reads it; the core already streams progress and gives up a call whose caller hangs up (`call.core.abandon`), so the door keeps the call's reply subject open instead of its caller.
- #1122: **Lead (b), "not installed, run X":** where the `se` binary is not on the machine there is no door to answer; that answer belongs to the entry the host spawns, which #1123 writes (a wrapper that starts `se mcp` or else serves the one tool saying how to install it).
- #1122: **Lead (a), typed arguments:** `args` stay strings as the usage shows. Next step: an optional typed args table in `component.toml`, rendered into each command's schema, additively.
- #1122: **R4, narrowed:** the catalog check refuses a command declared read-only without an MCP description, the commands the door offers; any other command, offered by no tool, needs none. Requiring one of all 57 commands would be text no host reads. The refusal lives in the door's check (`mcp.Tools`) and SE's generator, not kern's declaration readers: there it would be a newly required field, a major release, against the issue's "minor". Next step if wanted: a work issue to require it in `index` and `kern/python/kern/declaration.py` (major), or to offer batch commands as a not-read-only tool.
- #1123: None of the requirements. No session has a Copilot token, so nothing was run against a real Copilot; the launcher and door were proven against the built `se` and recorded shapes only. ### Not delivered: needs a Copilot run For the Monday checklist in #1124: 1. Whether VS Code reads `.github/mcp.json`, and under which key: the file uses the Copilot CLI's `mcpServers` with `"tools": ["*"]`; VS Code's own `.vscode/mcp.json` uses `servers`. 2. Whether a plugin install (Copilot CLI's, and VS Code's "Install Plugin from Source") picks `.github/plugin/plugin.json` over `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, and reads its `agents`, `skills`, `hooks` and `mcpServers` fields as written. 3. `copilot mcp get se` in a checkout of SE, and in another repository with the plugin installed: the server is listed and its tools are `status`, `greeting`, `integration`, `overhaul`, `tickets`. 4. Whether Copilot expands `${PLUGIN_ROOT}` in a plugin's MCP `args` (the Copilot manifest uses it; Claude's manifest keeps `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`). 5. The working directory a host starts a project MCP server in: `.github/mcp.json` runs `bash scripts/se_mcp.sh --root .`, relative to the repository root. 6. That `se/*` in a Copilot agent's strict `tools` list admits the door's tools, and that they show as `se/status` and so on. 7. The worker launch line of "Host procedure under Copilot": that `copilot` takes `-C`, `--trust-folder`, `--no-ask-user`, `--allow-tool=<list>`, `-n` and `-p` as written, which `--allow-tool` names the worker's shell, edits and the `se` server take, and that the run goes on detached; and its Windows `Start-Process` twin. 8. That the launcher's stand-in door (binary missing) is accepted by Copilot's client: it echoes the client's `protocolVersion` and answers `initialize`, `tools/list`, `tools/call` and `ping`. 9. That `se_mcp.ps1` passes standard input and output through to the binary under a real host (`Start-Process -NoNewWindow`): its tests cover only the stand-in door, as a fake binary cannot be a script on Windows. 10. That `bash` is on the PATH of a Windows Copilot host for the MCP command (Git for Windows), as the Copilot hooks already assume. 11. That the greeting's `SE runs without kern` line reaches a Copilot session through `sessionStart`'s `additionalContext`, and the se agent says it in its first message.
- #1124: **A plugin's MCP server starts in the plugin's folder.** Copilot CLI 1.0.112's `copilot mcp get se --json`, in another repository with SE installed by `se@se` or `--plugin-dir`: `"cwd": "${PLUGIN_ROOT}"`. So the declaration's `--root .` names the plugin's folder, not the project, and in another repository `se-status` would answer about that folder (an installed plugin has no `.git`: `no-checkout`). The self-test reports it as a `noted` line; `docs/copilot.md` names the workaround, `copilot mcp add se -- bash <se>/scripts/se_mcp.sh --root <repo>`. Next step: a work issue for the door to learn the project from the host, the MCP `roots` the client offers (in `kern/mcp`, #1142's area, or the launcher), and the same question for the hooks' `bash scripts/...` with `"cwd": "."` in another repository. Not done here: it is a kern or launcher change beyond this issue.
- #1124: **The plugin's `.github/mcp.json` wins over the Copilot manifest's `mcpServers`.** With both files in the plugin, `mcp get se` shows the project file's entry (`scripts/se_mcp.sh`, no `${PLUGIN_ROOT}`); removing it shows the manifest's. Harmless today, as both run the launcher from the plugin's folder; it makes #1123's point 4 (`${PLUGIN_ROOT}` expansion) moot for the CLI.

Only plugin@marketplace installs will be supported in a future release.` The marketplace route `se@se` works from the generated `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`; README lists it first.

- #1279: **Live fast-forward test, the first landing after the flip (integrator step):** 1. Run the usual `work_tickets.py integrate <pull request>` (without gh: its hand-off, then `integrate --read-back …`). Check that its output has `Landing commit: <sha> on <base>, mb <mb> as author and committer` and `Fast-forward: origin's main moved from <base> to <sha>`, and that the plan has no `merge_pull_request` step. 2. Run `git fetch origin main && git rev-parse origin/main`: it equals that `<sha>`, and equals the landing pull request's head, the commit its `check` run names. 3. Run `git log -1 --format='%an <%ae>|%cn <%ce>%n%B' origin/main`: the first line is `mb <mb>|mb <mb>`, and the body holds no `@` address and no `Co-authored-by:`. Paste this output on the work issue (acceptance 2). 4. Run `git rev-list --parents -1 origin/main`: one parent, the previous main. 5. Check that the landing pull request reads as merged (`pull_request_read get`: `merged: true`) and that main's push `check` run on `<sha>` succeeded. 6. If origin refuses the push, integrate stops naming the ruleset change, and main stays where it was. Do the person step, then integrate again.
- #1279: **Acceptance 2 (post-merge):** the output of step 3 above.

Moving them anyway would need a content change to clean commits; the owner decides whether "moved" means more than "names its rewritten commit".

With `component = "forms"` added it is refused as a second instance of the component (kern `index.Wiring.Merge` only appends; the catalog check allows one instance per component). In a project without a `wiring.toml`, any overlay also makes `cli.Source` serve the project's own wiring, so SE's tools leave the door, `forms` with them. Next step, for SE: decide whether kern's overlay may switch off an instance of the wiring it merges into (a kern change, outside this package), then add the README line.
- #1507: **R4, A2 and the Verification's first block, SE's own value:** SE's own default waits on the owner's answer, so the branch ships `claude_mcp = false`. Once the owner says yes: set `claude_mcp = true` in `se.toml` `[plugin]`, rewrite the comment's last sentence, run `uv run python scripts/se_plugin.py build` and commit the rebuilt `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`. Every test that pins SE's value reads it from se.toml, so nothing else changes.
- #1509: None. The four older tickets that the gate would refuse at their next edit stay as they are, since requirement 2 edits none.
- #1515: **R6, SE's value `on`:** the survey found four open issues SE's own commands filed from a template that the backstop would label (#1172, #1286, #1358 against `ticket`, #1376 against `work`), so `se.toml` stays unchanged as R6 says, and the Verification's `backstop == "on"` line and its `se_switches.py` grep fail. Next steps: SE fixes those four bodies (each finding is listed above), or decides they may be labelled, and then a follow-up sets `[forms] backstop = "on"` in `se.toml` with a comment naming README "Forms".
- #1530: **Acceptance 1, ownership recorded on the issue:** the acceptance asks that the issue record the paths; a worker writes no issue, so they are recorded in row 1 above and in the ticket before code. Next step: SE copies row 1 into the issue if it wants it there.
- #1568: None. The person's steps are prepared below, under "## Owner step", not taken: a worker may not take them. Integrator: escalate them to SE as one step only the person approves (work model, Steps only the person approves).
- #1571: **The real read of kern by the docs workflow's token was not run.** This container's GitHub GraphQL read answers 403 for every repository, and GitHub access to kern is not enabled for this session, so whether kern is public could not be checked. The first hourly run of `.github/workflows/docs.yml` after landing shows it: either a kern lane, or the gap line "Not shown: <owner>/kern could not be read (…)" and a stderr warning in the "Build the Timeline" step.
- #1571: **Access needed if kern is private (escalation):** the workflow's `github.token` reaches only SE's own repository, besides public ones. The smallest read access is a token limited to kern with read-only **Issues** and **Contents** (tags; Metadata comes with it), e.g. a fine-grained personal access token or a GitHub App installation token, stored as an SE repository secret and passed as `GITHUB_TOKEN` to the "Build the Timeline" step; or kern made public, which needs nothing. Only the owner can create the secret; the workflow change is one line once it exists.
- #1579: None. Requirement 3's time of day, listed here at first delivery, is settled by SE's decision on the work issue: the snapshot is named by date only.
- #1582: None of this issue's requirements. The comment's last sentence, "Required components that cannot be found stop the start, as SE#1589 designs", is left to SE#1589, which owns that design: this branch only reports offers and never stops the start.
- #1621: **Filing through the tracker (requirement 1, "or file").** Neither fill files an issue, a comment or a message itself. `templates.py fill` writes a non-file template's document to `--out` or stdout. The agent files it through the filing commands or the GitHub tools, which the template gate checks. The tool's `fill` writes nothing at all, because kern's MCP door offers read-only tools alone (README "The MCP door": "no command that writes"). Next steps: 1. kern: a door tool that writes (see Tickets for other repositories). 2. Then the tool's `fill` writes a claimed file. 3. A fill whose template's action is `issue.create` hands its document to that action's filing command (`work_tickets.py file`, `backlog file`, `route`), through kern's relay in the cloud. SE re-scopes this or files it as the next piece.
- #1623: None. Flake already at base, not this branch's: `tests/test_se_templates.py::test_a_gate_past_its_bound_passes_and_logs` failed once in a focused run under `-n 4`. Run 200 times, 8 at once: at `origin/main` (`feb1048`) 1 failed and 199 passed; on this branch 2 failed and 198 passed. It waits on a zero-second bound, so the order of two threads decides it.
- #1638: None. Requirement 4's escalation is replaced by the person's decision, as the issue's comment says; nothing is escalated.
- #1667: None. The contents API's latency is not measured: this session's proxy answered GitHub's API with 403 (see Spec gaps); the design's calls table says so.
- #1673: None. The real command was not run against GitHub: `work_tickets.py release` on this repository would cut SE's next release, which only SE asks for. Its landing reuses `integrate`'s landing plan and read-back, which the integrator runs against GitHub on every landing.
- #1677: **A red `main` and a worker stopped by the usage limit are not counted.** The issue's Approach names them as failures, but neither leaves a mark on an issue the countdown reads; escalations and refused landings are counted, as both add the `escalated` label. Next step: a label or comment those two events write on their work issue, which `retro_countdown` and the Go port then count.
- #1677: **`work_tickets.py suite` before and after was not run.** It refused: "/home/user/SE still holds the ignored output of 2 folder(s) whose tracked files git deleted … components/forms, kern", stale `__pycache__` folders from the container's cached snapshot; the host's permission check refused `git clean -fdX` on them. The integrator's complete suite measures it.
- #1677: **No run against real GitHub.** gh is not signed in here; `retro.py due` and the filing check were run against fakes only (gh door, relay door and plan path), not against the tracker.
- #1679: None. Requirement 2's `help` and `components list` are kern's verbs; their budget test is the kern ticket below, as this repository cannot test them.
- #1683: **Requirement 1, the rule `dangling` in Go.** It finds an SE command that does not exist through `scripts/references.py`, which reads the checkout's scripts and their argument parsers. Go runs no such check, so a Go fill passes a text the Python engine refuses for a dangling command; the shared cases hold no such text. Next: port the command and subcommand discovery to Go (or have the component ask a Python verb for it), then add dangling texts to `tests/template_cases.py`.
- #1683: **Requirement 1, five adapters' checkers in Go.** `field-report` (`work_tickets:field_report_problems`), `merge-message` (`work_tickets:merge_release_kind`), `review` (`review:checked_report`), `retro` and `overhaul` (`*:judgment_problems`) stay Python; Go's check of them refuses, naming the checker and `scripts/templates.py`. Only `deck:pugh_problems` is ported, so a decision's Pugh node checks in Go. Next: port each with its cases, one per package.
- #1683: **Requirement 1, template repositories in Go.** The component reads its checkout's `templates/` from the working tree; `catalog`'s resolution through se.toml `[templates] repositories` and the read at origin's integration branch stay Python.
- #1683: **kern's targets.** The GitHub door's writing tools and the files module accepting only a filled template are kern's changes, drafted below; SE's filing commands and gate still check after the fact until they land.
- #1758: None. The release after landing (the issue's Approach) is the integrator's; this branch changes the se binary, so it is `se major`.
- #1773: None. CI's `check` on a fresh runner is this pull request's own run, which the landing reads.
- #1786: None. The docs workflow's run on main can only be read after the landing (see the evidence table).
- #1790: **Acceptance, two checks on the test project:** after the steps, `se_modules.py breaking` fails (`the releases of se (upgrade-test-project -> se) could not be read: origin: <owner> marks no repository se as a component`), and `se_workflows.py check` exits 2 (`SE's version range ... is not locked yet`).
- #1790: Cause: SE's repository carries no topic `component`. GitHub's search for the owner's repositories with that topic lists `obs-html-style-generic`, `obs-html`, `kern` and `obs` only.
- #1790: So no project can lock `[components.se]`. The run used `[paths] se` in `~/.se/local.toml`, the remedy the guide's SE 8 now names, and a one-off `ref` pin for `se_workflows.py write`.
- #1790: Next step, the person's: add the topic `component` to SE's repository, then rerun the guide's checks.
- #1790: **`se_workflows.py write` ignores `--ref`** while `[components.se]` is an unlocked range: it refuses `is not locked yet` before reading the option. It is a separate SE fix, outside this issue.
- #1790: **README.md is 14 bytes under hygiene's 200 KB limit** after main's boot section: the next README addition needs a split first.
- #1790: **The boot reading the sections** (comment item 3) is SE#1719's boot. This branch delivers the reader it calls, `upgrade_guide.py show`.
- #1790: **The components' own guides** (comment item 2) are other repositories' work: tickets below.
- #1790: **`components/go.mod`'s comment** `(se.toml [modules.kern])` stays stale: editing that file moves the release guard's inputs for a comment.

## 4. The standing questions

<!-- matrix: The matrix: each standing question against each chapter, a cell naming what answers it there; collect fills the field reports' answers, the judge the rest. -->

Judge: chapter 21 (24 Nov, outside the work issues) answers "Has the process gone stale?" (the sync run as an audit), "What would have stopped this from being produced at all?" (a refusal of raw filing; a plain guard line), "What did the shell do that a command could have done?" (the mirror sync) and "Which rule, prompt or tool made each bad output?" ## Answers to the standing questions

<!-- answers: The retro's answer to each standing question over the whole window, one `### <question>` each; "None" where nothing applies. -->

### What came back from real use since the last retro?

<!-- real_use: What the person or a real run sent back: a refusal, a failure, a request. In a field report: what came back from running the work for real. "None" where nothing applies. -->

The person's comment on this issue: a routine mirror sync expanded into exploration and validation before the delta was compared, and a refreshed checkout did not refresh the host's loaded agent or MCP state (MCP still asked for binary 0.14.0 with SE 13.1.0 installed). From the field reports: three cloud intakes failed on a missing kern module (#1757, #1758, #1773); obs-html 0.5.0 needed an obs install SE's lock lacked (#1773); the upgrade guide's first real run sent back three faults (#1790).


### Which scripts did the AI write that a machine could own?

<!-- machine_scripts: Each ad hoc script or command line written more than once, and the component that could own it. "None" where nothing applies. -->

The mirror sync, a machine-local script the person runs often (`sync_public.py`), which collect does not see, so "Ad hoc scripts to promote" reads None. In the reports: ticket evidence written with inline Python twice (#1752, #1757; `move --to done --evidence`); the guard's sentence list pasted by hand (#1751, #1752; `field-report --draft`); a guide's checks as scratch shell (#1790; `upgrade_guide.py check`). Repetition argues for a command; it does not by itself argue for Go or a new component.


### Anything worth porting to other projects, or so bad we remove it?

<!-- port_or_remove: What other projects should take over, and what should go. "None" where nothing applies. -->

Port: `own`/`sections` of `scripts/templates.py` to other documents (#1678); the upgrade guide's shape to obs, obs-html and kern (#1790). Remove: SE's Python render of kern's templates, done (#1758). Not picked: a separate Go "agent tools" component beside kern; kern already places components, streams progress and serves them through the `se` MCP tools this session uses.


### Has the process gone stale?

<!-- stale_process: A step that no longer earns its cost, or a rule nobody follows. "None" where nothing applies. -->

Retro findings are no longer filed after a retro: all 12 of #864's stayed unnamed. A hand-filed retro stayed open 19 days outside the flow. The relay snapshot trusted up to 10 minutes (#1790).


### What would have stopped this from being produced at all?

<!-- not_produced: The check, gate or rule that would have prevented the waste before it was made. "None" where nothing applies. -->

A gate that refuses the next filing until the last retro's work findings are named or declined; a refusal of raw `gh issue create` in an SE session; a greeting line that says plainly that SE starts sessions; a mirror-sync command with bounded phases.


### What did the shell do that a command could have done? Name it.

<!-- shell_command: Each shell sequence an SE command could run, by its name. "None" where nothing applies. -->

The mirror sync (a command of SE's own); `se_modules.py sync kern` before intake (#1757, #1758, #1773; intake could place kern itself); `obs-components install` of the docs style (#1757); `pr-body` plus `update_pull_request` without gh (#1751).


### Did anything local earn promotion upstream?

<!-- promote_upstream: A local helper, rule or note that belongs in SE or in kern. "None" where nothing applies. -->

The machine-local mirror sync into SE, beside `work_tickets.py parity`. `se_modules.go_version` into kern's release docs (#1758). `upgrade_guide.py` to kern's tool belt (#1790).


### For each failed check, what rule would have caught it?

<!-- failed_checks: Each failed check or test, and the rule that would have caught it earlier. "None" where nothing applies. -->

The 5 `work-issue` breaks are release landings, which name no work issue by design: the rule check should skip them. The 6 `check` failures (#1409, #1411, #1614, #1669, #1729, #1770) and the field reports' failures each name an existing rule run late (docs build, Go suite for `templates/`, hygiene in pre-commit).


### What goes well, and what makes it go well?

<!-- goes_well: What worked, and the cause that made it work. "None" where nothing applies. -->

249 of 256 closed issues landed, 12 escalated, none duplicated; refusals that name file, line and remedy made each fix one edit (#1751, #1757); building at a moved pin first (#1758); probing the real tool before choosing (#1786); research agents in parallel with a source line per change (#1790). Most of retro #864's fixes reached the tree even though no issue named them.


### What goes badly, each with its moment in the log?

<!-- goes_badly: What went wrong, each with the log line, commit or comment where it shows. "None" where nothing applies. -->

24 Nov: the mirror sync run as an audit (about 20 min); #1811 filed raw around the open-retro gate; the retro run inline; collect about 20 min with no progress. 13 to 23 Nov: kern missing at cloud intake three times; suite over budget five times (about 300 s against 120 to 240 s); `se_start.py status` over its 1 s budget eleven times.


### What do the thoughts say that the actions do not?

<!-- thoughts_actions: Where the reasoning and what was done part ways. "None" where nothing applies. -->

The SE session's reasoning held that it could not start sessions while its host offered `create_session`; the greeting's `start_sessions = true: off` reads as "starting is off".


### Start, stop, keep, more, less.

<!-- start_stop: One line each: what to start, stop, keep, do more and do less. "None" where nothing applies. -->

Start: one command for the mirror sync; progress lines in every command past its budget; filing every retro finding right after the retro. Stop: raw `gh issue create` in SE sessions; running roles inline. Keep: refusals that name their remedy; building first at a moved pin. More: bounded parallel reads. Less: sequential GitHub reads in collect.


### Which rule, prompt or tool made each bad output, and what change stops it?

<!-- bad_output_cause: Each bad output, the rule, prompt or tool behind it, and the change, with its target file. "None" where nothing applies. -->

The misread guard line: `scripts/copilot_guards.py` `state()` prints the guard's state, not the behaviour; it names the behaviour instead. The inline retro: `src/agents/se.md` says "You start no session yourself ... unless", the inverse of the built-in default; it states the default. The raw filing: the gate lives only in `work_tickets.py file`; a hook refusal stops the bypass. The 20-minute collect: `scripts/retro.py` reads GitHub one call at a time; bounded parallel reads and a progress line. The unfiled findings: nothing gates SE's filing after a retro; `scripts/work_tickets.py file` refuses until they are named.


## 5. Classes and homes

<!-- classes: One cause and one fix per class, its home the first that holds in the order check, refusal, gate, prefill, checklist line, command, setting, sentence, and each class checked against the tree: fixed, past or open. -->

Judge: retro #864's five classes stand as measured in step 2: Runner-only test failures, Rules found late, Cloud path by hand and First runs of the retro tools are fixed in their homes; Sessions stopped unseen is fixed (Stop hook, limit restart) with 2 stray issues. The tree reads them `open` only because #864 gave them no home or mark. The hand-filed retro of 5 Nov: fixed or past, as step 2 says. This retro's classes follow, with their findings in the block.

#### Retro debt carried forward

Cause: a retro closes without its findings filed or other open retros closed, so debt outlives the run (12 unfiled findings, one retro open 19 days). Fix: collect lists every other open retro or overhaul issue and every earlier work finding no issue names; file closes the open ones as covered, and the next filing waits until the findings are named or declined. Home: gate, `scripts/retro.py`. Tree: open.

#### Routine operations run as audits

Cause: the mirror sync lives in a machine-local script and personal instructions, so an agent without them improvises. Fix: one SE command that runs the sync in its phases and stops only where there is something to replay. Home: command, `scripts/work_tickets.py`. Tree: open.

#### Host state lags the checkout

Cause: an update changes files but not the host's loaded agent and MCP state, and the report shows one version. Fix: the start report names source, binary, running core and the host's loaded version apart and says when the host must reload. Home: check, `scripts/se_start.py`. Tree: open.

#### Filing gate bypassed by hand

Cause: the open-retro gate lives only in SE's filing command; a raw `gh issue create` passes. Fix: a hook refuses raw issue creation in an SE session, naming `work_tickets.py file`. Home: refusal, `scripts/se_hook.sh`. Tree: open.

#### Guard line read as its opposite

Cause: the greeting prints the guard's state (`start_sessions = true: off`), which reads as "starting sessions is off", and se.md states the default inversely. Fix: the line names the behaviour ("SE starts sessions"), and se.md states the default. Home: prefill, `scripts/copilot_guards.py`. Tree: open.

#### Long commands run silent and serial

Cause: SE's long commands (collect, integrate, suite, sync) run as scripts outside kern's streaming and side-by-side calls, reading one call at a time; collect and the backlog-file due check read the repository's whole closed history and filter it client-side, a design built against small fakes that no test or review measured. Fix: no time limit; every long command reports its phase, done/total where known, elapsed time and stalls or failures, asks only for its window, and reads independent inputs in bounded parallel, writes in order, through kern's stream and the `se` MCP server where they serve it. Home: check, `scripts/se_components.py`. Tree: open.

#### Rule checks count release landings

Cause: the `work-issue` check counts release landings, which name no work issue. Fix: it skips them. Home: check, `scripts/retro.py`. Tree: open.

#### Retro doc outgrows the size limit

Cause: the block carries every issue's facts; 256 issues make about 370 KB against the `large` check's 200 KB (`MAX_BYTES`, `LARGE_ALLOWED`). Fix, decided by the person: ephemeral list reports (retro docs, collect output, reports) may exceed the cap; permanent documentation stays under it and is cleaned up. Home: check, `scripts/hygiene.py`. Tree: fixed by #1812.

#### Cloud intake lacks kern

Cause: intake needs kern's SDK and a cloud start had not placed it (#1757, #1758, #1773). Fix: intake places kern at its pin first. Home: command, `scripts/work_tickets.py`. Tree: open.


#### Messages queue behind a running agent

Cause: SE queued three follow-ups to this running retro agent; they sat unread over 10 minutes. Fix: SE steers a running agent and queues only to an idle one. Home: sentence, `src/skills/work-model/SKILL.md`. Tree: open.

Judged through the person's principle, 24 Nov: "Everything that has to be done repeatedly and is mechanical needs to be taken off the plate of the agent." Every finding's home is a check, refusal, gate, prefill or command where one can hold it: the debt gate (1, 2), the sync command (3), the start report (4), the raw-filing hook (6), the guard line (7), collect's window and progress (10, 11), the retro-due trigger (16). Two homes stay sentences because no command can carry them yet: se.md's session default (8), which the guard line (7) makes mechanical where it matters, and steering a running agent (17), which is a choice of the host's send tool.

Home order, strongest first: check, refusal, gate, prefill, checklist line, command, setting, sentence. A class's `home_kind` is the first of them that holds; its `home` is the file the fix changes, its `mark` a text the fix leaves there, which the tree check looks for.

The classes of retro #864 against the tree:

| class | home | tree |
|---|---|---|
| Runner-only test failures |  `` | open |
| Rules found late |  `` | open |
| Cloud path by hand |  `` | open |
| Sessions stopped unseen |  `` | open |
| First runs of the retro tools |  `` | open |

## 6. The person decides

<!-- decisions: Every open class, decided by the person in an SE session; SE files what is accepted. -->

The retro agent produces the report; SE and the person go through it together and SE files the issues (the person, 24 Nov). Nothing below is decided by the person except finding 14.

- Decisions, each recommended, not yet decided by the person:
  - 1, retro debt: Settle. Collect lists other open retro or overhaul runs and unnamed earlier findings, file closes the open ones as covered, the first filing after a retro waits until every work finding is named or declined.
  - 5, restart after an update: Restart. The session restarts itself through the host's session tool and resumes; where the host cannot, an honest handoff naming every version.
  - 6, raw issue creation: Hook. A pre-tool hook refuses raw issue creation in an SE checkout, naming SE's filing command.
  - 9, agent tools: Per command. A command moves onto kern when a retro shows it run repeatedly or costly, served by the `se` MCP server; no new umbrella component.
  - 11, progress: Contract. No time limit; every long command reports phase, done/total, elapsed time and stalls; independent reads in bounded parallel; a check holds it.
- Decided by the person: 14, ephemeral list reports may exceed the size cap; fixed by #1812.
- Work findings 2, 3, 4, 7, 8, 10, 12, 13, 15, 16, 17: proposed, for SE and the person to file.
- Task: the second retro issue, filed by hand on 5 Nov on another tracker of the same tree, is covered by this run; closing it is the person's.
- The deck with the five decision notes is kept outside the repository, with the retro agent's session.


<!-- se:retro-judge -->
## Judgment

The judge writes the person's field feedback into step 1, fills what steps 3 and 4 leave open, answers every standing question, fills `findings`, `classes` and `went_well` in the block below, then runs `retro.py file 1810 --draft <this file>`, which refuses a step or a question left open: a finding has `title` (five words or more), `body`, `class` (a class's `name`), `home` (the file it changes), `controversial` (true where it changes a rule, trades one metric for another, the judge is unsure, or it recurred after an earlier fix) with its `why`, and optionally `check` (a rule check: work-issue, check, outside-pr, field-report); a class has `name`, `cause`, `fix`, `home` and `home_kind` (one of: check, refusal, gate, prefill, checklist line, command, setting, sentence), and optionally `status`, `pattern`, `limits`, `mark`; `went_well` lists what went well. Step 6 is the person's, in the session. This part is not posted.
<!-- se:retro-judge:end -->

      "not_delivered": [
        "**A flaky host test, from before this branch:** `TestAClientStopsTheCore` (`kern/host/host_test.go`) fails about once in 100 to 500 runs with `call.core.stop: nats: connection closed`, at `729eaed` as on this branch (`go test -count=500 -run TestAClientStopsTheCore ./host/`: 1, 1 and 3 failures at base, 3, 2 and 1 here). The core answers the stop, then shuts the bus down before the server has delivered the answer to the client. Remedy: on stop, wait until the asker has had the answer, for example by closing the bus only after the client's connection is gone or after a short drain, then hold the test at `-count=1000`.",
        "**`call.` does not stream yet:** the design says `call.<command>` streams progress lines, with `working, nothing to show yet` after half a second. Here the lines arrive with the final answer, since a component's reply on `cmd.<i>.>` is one message (`Response: MaxMsgs 1`). Next: a progress subject the instance may publish on, which the core forwards to the caller's inbox."
      ],

        "**The exact post-merge landing check**, once a fresh cloud session installs an SE whose hooks post to kern (the environment question, with the owner) and this has landed: start a worker in the cloud on a small work issue; when it delivers, run the integrator; `integrate <pull request>` with **no** `--pr-description`/`--issue-body`/`--branch`, confirm it reads the pull request and work issue from the relay (its output names no saved file, and `.se/.runtime/relay/` holds the answer files it wrote); run the plan's tool calls; `integrate --read-back <branch> --landing-pull <n> --check-run <url>` with **no** `--landing-description`, confirm it reads the landing description from the relay and closes the issue.         "**`integrate`'s read-back as one call (requirement 1, \"the read-back runs as a continuation\").** Today the hand-off is still `integrate <pull>` then `integrate --read-back`, now with no retyped file between them.       "retro": {},
      "not_delivered": [
        "**Issue-worthy, for the dispatcher (I file no issues):**",
        "kern tells a component nothing when it abandons a call (a request failed, the core ended), so a streamed command's thread (`Stepped`) waiting on a step lives until `STEP_ANSWER_WAIT` (65 min). The old rule that a new call of the command gives that thread up would kill a call running beside it, so it is gone (Removed behaviour). Such a thread now holds no directory while it waits. A `cmd.<i>.<command>` stop word from the core when `CallSaying` ends a call that still has a continuation would end it at once.",
        "The process has one working directory. A call in another directory waits until the calls in the current one end. An interactive routed call waits at most `CALL_WAIT` (60 s) and then runs directly. A call kept waiting longer is not starved forever, but nothing orders the waiters.",
        "`kern/README.md`'s `cmd.<i>.<command>` row still says a step answers `lines`, which PR #1048 removed; out of this issue's scope, left as is.",
        "`gofmt -l` flags `kern/components/github/github_test.go` at main; not touched here."
      ],
      "at": "2026-11-13T16:00:05Z"
    },

      "not_delivered": [
        "**Issue-worthy, for the dispatcher (I file no issues):**",
        "A caller killed outright (its session ends, the core lives on for another session) sends no `call.core.abandon`; its call runs until it ends or the core ends. \"The session ending\" is covered where the core ends: the core gives every call up (`ErrCoreEnds`), and its bus closing ends the component processes. A per-caller liveness check would need the bus's system events.",
        "A request already out runs on when its call is given up (`index.serve` drops its answer): the github component's `step` verb keeps its outbox entry until its step-wait (60 min) or an answer, so the session may still be pointed at a step nobody waits for. A `Serve` that takes a context would drop it at once.",
        "A script's main already running when its call is given up runs to its end (Python cannot stop a thread); only waits (a step's answer, the directory line) end at once."
      ],

        "**A plugin's MCP server starts in the plugin's folder.** Copilot CLI 1.0.112's `copilot mcp get se --json`, in another repository with SE installed by `se@se` or `--plugin-dir`: `\"cwd\": \"${PLUGIN_ROOT}\"`. So the declaration's `--root .` names the plugin's folder, not the project, and in another repository `se-status` would answer about that folder (an installed plugin has no `.git`: `no-checkout`). The self-test reports it as a `noted` line; `docs/copilot.md` names the workaround, `copilot mcp add se -- bash <se>/scripts/se_mcp.sh --root <repo>`. Next step: a work issue for the door to learn the project from the host, the MCP `roots` the client offers (in `kern/mcp`, #1142's area, or the launcher), and the same question for the hooks' `bash scripts/...` with `\"cwd\": \".\"` in another repository. Not done here: it is a kern or launcher change beyond this issue.",
        "**The plugin's `.github/mcp.json` wins over the Copilot manifest's `mcpServers`.** With both files in the plugin, `mcp get se` shows the project file's entry (`scripts/se_mcp.sh`, no `${PLUGIN_ROOT}`); removing it shows the manifest's. Harmless today, as both run the launcher from the plugin's folder; it makes #1123's point 4 (`${PLUGIN_ROOT}` expansion) moot for the CLI.",
        "**Direct installs are deprecated.** `copilot plugin install mb-89/SE` prints `Warning: Direct plugin installs (repos, URLs, local paths) are deprecated. Only plugin@marketplace installs will be supported in a future release.` The marketplace route `se@se` works from the generated `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`; README lists it first.",
        "**The door drops answers when its input ends at once.** Piping requests into `se mcp` and closing standard input right after gives no answer at all (exit 0); with input held open it answers. No host does the former, so no Copilot effect; a client test that pipes a file would see nothing."
      ],

Land the setting first, by a housekeeping pull request of the owner: `[tests] ci = false` in se.toml. While the ruleset still requires `check`, which never runs there, the owner merges it by hand. 3. In the integration branch's ruleset, replace the required check `check` with the required status `se integrator verdict`. 4. Deliver a small work pull request, then from the main checkout: `uv run python <se>/scripts/work_tickets.py integrate <n> --worktree <checkout>`. It should print `The integrator's status se integrator verdict: success written on <head>`, `Landing pull request opened: <url>`, `Verdict se integrator verdict: success (the integrator <login>'s status ...)` and `Landed: <commit> on main`. 5. `gh api --hostname <host> repos/<owner>/<repository>/commits/<landing head>/statuses` should list the status: context `se integrator verdict`, state `success`, description `passed tree <tree> suite <40 hex digits>`, creator the integrator.",
        "**Requirement 5, narrowed: the host comes from origin through `remote_repo`, not `scripts/se_home.py`.** The status goes through the landing's existing door, which every landing step already reaches through `remote_repo` (origin). Rerouting that door through `se_home.py` would change every landing, the ones with CI too, against the dispatch's rule that a landing with the mode off stays as it is. Next step: route `remote_repo`'s readers through `se_home.py` as one change of its own, for every landing.",
        "**The mode in the hand-off and through kern's relay.** The host's GitHub tools write no commit status, so with `ci = false` and `gh` not signed in `integrate` refuses before it merges anything (`test_without_ci_a_hand_off_refuses_before_it_merges_anything`). A cloud landing without CI needs a GitHub tool that writes a commit status, or a step through kern's relay; kern is frozen until SE#1172 closes, so this waits."

Keep \"Require status checks to pass\" with `check`, \"Require linear history\", \"Block force pushes\" and \"Restrict deletions\".         "**Live fast-forward test, the first landing after the flip (integrator step):** 1. Check that its output has `Landing commit: <sha> on <base>, mb <mb> as author and committer` and `Fast-forward: origin's main moved from <base> to <sha>`, and that the plan has no `merge_pull_request` step. 2. Run `git fetch origin main && git rev-parse origin/main`: it equals that `<sha>`, and equals the landing pull request's head, the commit its `check` run names. 3. Run `git log -1 --format='%an <%ae>|%cn <%ce>%n%B' origin/main`: the first line is `mb <mb>|mb <mb>`, and the body holds no `@` address and no `Co-authored-by:`. Paste this output on the work issue (acceptance 2). 4. Run `git rev-list --parents -1 origin/main`: one parent, the previous main. 5. Check that the landing pull request reads as merged (`pull_request_read get`: `merged: true`) and that main's push `check` run on `<sha>` succeeded. 6. If origin refuses the push, integrate stops naming the ruleset change, and main stays where it was. Do the person step, then integrate again.",

        "**R8, the off switch:** not delivered and no README line written, because the switch the issue names does not work against main. A `.se/wiring.toml` with `[instances.forms]` `enabled = false` is refused (\"instance forms names no component\"). With `component = \"forms\"` added it is refused as a second instance of the component (kern `index.Wiring.Merge` only appends; the catalog check allows one instance per component). In a project without a `wiring.toml`, any overlay also makes `cli.Source` serve the project's own wiring, so SE's tools leave the door, `forms` with them. Next step, for SE: decide whether kern's overlay may switch off an instance of the wiring it merges into (a kern change, outside this package), then add the README line."

      "not_delivered": [
        "**Filing through the tracker (requirement 1, \"or file\").** Neither fill files an issue, a comment or a message itself. `templates.py fill` writes a non-file template's document to `--out` or stdout. The agent files it through the filing commands or the GitHub tools, which the template gate checks. The tool's `fill` writes nothing at all, because kern's MCP door offers read-only tools alone (README \"The MCP door\": \"no command that writes\"). Next steps: 1. kern: a door tool that writes (see Tickets for other repositories). 2. Then the tool's `fill` writes a claimed file. 3. A fill whose template's action is `issue.create` hands its document to that action's filing command (`work_tickets.py file`, `backlog file`, `route`), through kern's relay in the cloud. SE re-scopes this or files it as the next piece."
      ],
      "at": "2026-11-22T19:09:55Z"
    },

      "retro": {
        "real_use": "The guard ran for real on this checkout: it listed `components/forms` (one `.pyc` from the restored snapshot), removed it and passed. In this shallow clone (51 commits) `kern`'s deletion is outside the history, so `kern/python/kern/__pycache__` is not seen as a leftover at all here; the integrator's deeper clone did see it.",
        "machine_scripts": "A small inline Python script that writes evidence into tickets and checks their acceptance items; `work_tickets.py move --to done` could take `--evidence <text>`.",
        "not_produced": "The guard's own test fixture (`.venv`, `__pycache__` in a deleted folder) already held a `.pyc` in `adapters/html/src`; a case for a folder with only cache would have shown at SE#1662 that the remedy is a destructive clean of harmless files.",
        "shell_command": "`obs-components install obs-html-style-generic` had to be run by hand before `tests/test_docs.py` could pass: the session start could install the docs style the docs build needs.",
        "failed_checks": "`tests/test_docs.py` failed 3 tests at base and with the change: the obs style `obs-html-style-generic` was not installed in `/root/.config/obs`; after installing it, 22 passed. A start check that the docs build's components are installed would have caught it. `work_tickets.py intake` first failed with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'kern'` until `se_modules.py sync kern` placed kern.",
        "goes_well": "The guard was one small function with fakeable git, so the change and its tests stayed in process and fast (0.76 s).",
        "goes_badly": "Intake failed on the missing kern module (`se_relay.py` line 189) and the docs tests on the missing style: both container setup, not the change.",
        "start_stop": "Start: install kern and the docs style at session start. Stop: None. Keep: guards that name their remedy. More: in-process tests on real files under `tmp_path`. Less: remedies that need a destructive shell command.",
        "bad_output_cause": "The refusal of SE#1662 named `git clean -fdX` for harmless cache; the guard (`scripts/work_tickets.py` `refuse_leftover_folders`) now removes it itself."
      },
      "not_delivered": [],
