      "retro": {
        "real_use": "This session's restart on the installed se 0.14.0: `instance github has no port template-accept` (Spec gaps). Go itself: `go: errors parsing go.mod: require kern: version \"v2.1.0\" invalid: should be v0 or v1, not v2` on the first build at the pin, before any code.",
        "port_or_remove": "Remove: SE's Python render of kern's template JSON (`kern_template`, `kern_fields`) and its agreement test, done here. Port: the filled-template hand-off to any application that writes through kern's GitHub door.",
        "not_produced": "A check in kern's release that an application builds at the new major (kern's `template/` built at `v<major>`) would have caught the module-path problem before SE's move; ticketed above.",
        "shell_command": "Splitting one file's two hunks into two commits took a copy-and-restore of `wiring.toml` in the shell; `git add -p` is interactive and refused here.",
        "promote_upstream": "`se_modules.go_version` (Go's spelling of a require above major 1) belongs in kern's release docs; ticketed above.",
        "failed_checks": "The PR's `release / build` failed in `kern-at-pin`, whose own regex read go.mod's require beside `se_modules`' (two readers of one pin; a rule \"one reader per fact\", gen-002, would have caught it). `tests/test_runme.py` failed only in the complete suite (kern's RUNME copy gained a marker); a focused rule \"a moved kern pin runs every test that reads `.se/modules/kern`\" would have caught it in the focused step. `go build` at the pin (module path, see above) and `go vet` (`tmpl.Check undefined` in `kern_test.go`); the rule \"build first at a new pin\" caught both before any change.",
        "goes_well": "Building at the new pin first: it showed all three breaks (go.mod spelling, the removed form check, the tool list) in one run, and the Go template component's `fill`/`accept` already existed, so the change was wiring.",
        "goes_badly": "Intake failed first with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'kern'` until `se_modules.py sync kern` placed kern's clone; a cloud start had not placed it.",
        "start_stop": "Start: a kern release check that builds an application at the new major. Stop: None. Keep: build first at a moved pin. More: in-process Go tests over SE's real wiring.toml. Less: Python mirrors of kern's internals."
      },

        "machine_scripts": "Three probe scripts in the scratchpad that built a hand-edited Timeline note through `timeline.build` to see what obs accepts. A command that builds one note's Markdown through obs and prints its refusals (obs's own `obs-document` on a one-note vault) would own it.",

        "bad_output_cause": "The broken build: `goal()` in `scripts/timeline.py` deleted backticks to make plain text, and `text()` trusted backslashes against wikilinks; fixed in this branch (`spans()`, `quoted_links()`)."

      "number": 1790,
      "title": "upgrade-guide-in-repo",
      "state_reason": "completed",
      "duplicate": false,
      "landed": true,
      "refused": 0,
      "escalations": 1,
      "suite_seconds": 52.3,
      "landing_pull": 1795,
      "report_pull": 1794,
      "retro": {
        "real_use": "- The run of the guide on a project at 5.134 sent back three faults, each fixed in 172a0208: - `sync` refused the obs pin at SE 8; - the template load check assumed a `README.md`; - nothing could find se as a component.",
        "machine_scripts": "- `checks.sh` and `steps.sh` in the scratchpad: the guide's checks and steps, written out. `upgrade_guide.py` could own a `check` verb that runs a section's **Check:** commands in a project, so the boot runs them, not an agent.",
        "port_or_remove": "- The guide's shape and the release refusal, to obs, obs-html and kern (tickets above).",
        "stale_process": "- `work_tickets.py refresh` trusts a relay snapshot up to 10 minutes old, so a comment posted just after the snapshot goes unseen (Spec gaps); a refresh after a peer's notice could read afresh.",
        "not_produced": "- A release check that refused a major without an upgrade section from the first major on: the knowledge would have been written at each landing, not reconstructed from 13 pull requests.",
        "shell_command": "- Building a project at an old SE (`git archive v5.134.2`, a hand-written `se.lock`, `se_workflows.py write` of the old SE). A `se_new.py --at <version>` fixture would make it one command.",
        "promote_upstream": "- `upgrade_guide.py` itself, to kern's tool belt once the components keep guides of the same shape.",
        "failed_checks": "- `test_template_cases` and the Go resolver test failed after the refusal text moved from se 6 to se 8: the Python and Go engines each hold the text. One owner of the text, shared by both engines, would have changed it in one place.",
        "goes_well": "- Two research agents in parallel, one for SE's majors and one for the components, each writing notes with a source line per change. They made the guide checkable against the code.",
        "goes_badly": "- A research note filed PR 1785 under an unreleased SE 13. Release v12.0.1 (`bbeb6df5`) shows it shipped as a patch, and 7ec9322d moved it.",
        "thoughts_actions": "- None",
        "start_stop": "- Start: run each guide's steps on a project at the old version before delivering. - Stop: trusting a research note's version mapping without the release commit. - Keep: one test that every major has its section. - More: mechanical checks that name their file. - Less: checks that pass on any input.",
        "bad_output_cause": "- The template load check passed on a TOML-only `templates/`, because it reads no TOML. The guide pairs it with `ls templates/*.toml`. `scripts/templates.py check --tree` could refuse a `templates/*.toml` other than `terms.toml`."
      },
      "not_delivered": [
        "**Acceptance, two checks on the test project:** after the steps, `se_modules.py breaking` fails (`the releases of se (upgrade-test-project -> se) could not be read: origin: <owner> marks no repository se as a component`), and `se_workflows.py check` exits 2 (`SE's version range ... is not locked yet`).",
        "Cause: SE's repository carries no topic `component`. GitHub's search for the owner's repositories with that topic lists `obs-html-style-generic`, `obs-html`, `kern` and `obs` only.",
        "So no project can lock `[components.se]`. The run used `[paths] se` in `~/.se/local.toml`, the remedy the guide's SE 8 now names, and a one-off `ref` pin for `se_workflows.py write`.",
        "Next step, the person's: add the topic `component` to SE's repository, then rerun the guide's checks.",
        "**`se_workflows.py write` ignores `--ref`** while `[components.se]` is an unlocked range: it refuses `is not locked yet` before reading the option. It is a separate SE fix, outside this issue.",
        "**README.md is 14 bytes under hygiene's 200 KB limit** after main's boot section: the next README addition needs a split first.",
        "**The boot reading the sections** (comment item 3) is SE#1719's boot. This branch delivers the reader it calls, `upgrade_guide.py show`.",
        "**The components' own guides** (comment item 2) are other repositories' work: tickets below.",
        "**`components/go.mod`'s comment** `(se.toml [modules.kern])` stays stale: editing that file moves the release guard's inputs for a comment."
      ],
      "at": "2026-11-23T17:38:44Z"
    }

  "findings": [
    {
      "title": "One retro clears the whole retro debt",
      "body": "The person: \"doing one retro needs to clear the retro debt overall.\" Collect lists every other open retro or overhaul issue of the project and every work finding of earlier retros that no issue names; `retro.py file` closes the open ones as covered by its report, linking it, and the report carries the unfiled findings forward. Today: a hand-filed retro stayed open 19 days and #864's 12 findings were never named.",
      "class": "Retro debt carried forward",
      "home": "scripts/retro.py",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "It changes how a retro closes: one run now settles every open retro and carries earlier findings, the person's new rule."
    },
    {
      "title": "Next filing waits for retro findings",
      "body": "The first `work_tickets.py file` after a retro closes refuses until each of its work findings is named by an issue (`retro #<n> finding <k>`) or recorded as declined, naming the missing ones. #864's findings went unfiled and the next collect could score none.",
      "class": "Retro debt carried forward",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Mirror sync becomes one command",
      "body": "The person: \"you're not auditing; you're just merging.\" A sync of a mirror from the public line took about 20 minutes as an audit. Promote the machine-local sync into SE: report each repository's state, apply where no overlay exists (one squashed sync commit, landing, tree parity), stop only where files must be replayed. It reuses `work_tickets.py parity` and the landing step; no audit, no broad suite.",
      "class": "Routine operations run as audits",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Report source, binary, core, host versions",
      "body": "The person's comment: after an update MCP still asked for binary 0.14.0 while SE 13.1.0 and binary 1.1.0 were installed. The start report names the checkout, the binary, the running core and the host's loaded agent and MCP version apart, and says when the host must reload; a refreshed checkout is never reported as a restarted runtime.",
      "class": "Host state lags the checkout",
      "home": "scripts/se_start.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Host-aware restart and handoff design",
      "body": "The person: \"The agent needs to have a reset button where it just restarts, sends the initial prompt again, and cleans everything up.\" Design through the host adapter: keep the request and the completed phase, start a fresh session with the current agent and kern, verify version and workspace, resume without repeating writes, clean only what the operation owns; where the host cannot, say so and give the smallest handoff. (#1811 held this and was closed as filed outside the gate.)",
      "class": "Host state lags the checkout",
      "home": "docs/copilot.md",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "A design with host limits the judge cannot settle: how far a session may restart itself is the person's call."
    },
    {
      "title": "Refuse raw issue creation in SE sessions",
      "body": "24 Nov: the SE session filed #1811 with raw `gh issue create` while retro #1810 held the gate. A PreToolUse hook in SE's plugin refuses `gh issue create` (and the host's issue-create tool) in a checkout with SE, naming `work_tickets.py file`.",
      "class": "Filing gate bypassed by hand",
      "home": "scripts/se_hook.sh",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "It takes a tool away from every SE session, trading flexibility for the gate holding; an outside repository's filing must still pass."
    },

      "title": "Guard line names the behaviour",
      "body": "The greeting printed `[copilot] start_sessions = true: off, today's behaviour`; the SE session read it as \"starting sessions is off\" and ran this retro inline. `copilot_guards.state()` names the behaviour: \"SE starts sessions itself (`create_session`)\" or \"SE prints the line, the person starts it\".",
      "class": "Guard line read as its opposite",
      "home": "scripts/copilot_guards.py",

      "title": "se.md states the session default",
      "body": "`src/agents/se.md` says \"You start no session yourself ... unless\" the guard line reads true, the inverse of the built-in default `docs/copilot.md` owns. It states: SE starts worker, integrator, reviewer and retro sessions with the host's session tool, unless `[copilot] start_sessions = false`, and links docs/copilot.md for the setting.",
      "class": "Guard line read as its opposite",
      "home": "src/agents/se.md",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Agent tools reuse kern, not a new component",
      "body": "The person proposed one Go \"agent tools\" component with subcomponents, presented through MCP. kern already places components, runs calls side by side (#1053), streams progress (kern's `stream_test.go`) and serves SE's commands as the `se` MCP tools this session uses. Judge's proposal: no umbrella component; a script becomes (or joins) a kern component when the retro's evidence shows repeated use and a cost the script cannot cut, one command at a time, Go where it is part of the system (python-ad-hoc-go-system). Not picked: a new Go component beside kern, which would duplicate placement, wiring and the MCP door. It recurred: slow tools without updates came up in the 6 Nov feedback-within-a-second work (one second's feedback for the commands `feedback.INTERACTIVE` lists) and in retro #864 finding 11 (collect takes the window it is given). Neither held for this case: the first covered interactive commands only, never collect, integrate or suite; the second fixed where the window starts, not what collect reads. The person, 24 Nov: \"move the scripts to a 'agent tools' component in go with subcomponents for each, ideally presented via mcp\". Weighed against what exists: SE's binary, kern (placement, wiring, side-by-side calls, streamed progress) and the `se` MCP server already present SE commands as tools, so a second umbrella would duplicate them; each command a retro finds run repeatedly or costly moves onto kern as a component, served by the `se` MCP server.",
      "class": "Long commands run silent and serial",
      "home": "docs/go-core.md",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "The person proposed the opposite shape; it decides the architecture of every port."
    },
    {
      "title": "Collect reads only the window, in parallel",
      "body": "The person, 24 Nov: \"why does 'collect retro draft' take 6 minutes? this shouldnt be an ad-hoc script. everything the agent needs to do needs to be well-prepared\" and \"obviously only collect over the window. how was this ever constructed otherwise, is that not obvious?\" A design defect: `retro.py` `pages()` (line 291) pages through every closed issue and every closed pull request of the repository's whole history, one request after another, and drops those outside the window client-side; the backlog-file due check reads the same way. Why it was built so: the first collect (12 Nov) was written against fake doors whose fixtures hold a handful of issues, where reading everything costs nothing and is simplest to test; the window's start was then fixed (#864 finding 11) without asking what is read. Why no review caught it: tests and reviews checked what collect returns, never how many requests it makes, and no command's cost against the window's size is measured. Fix: ask GitHub for the window only (closed since its start, server-side), read the per-issue comments and checks in bounded parallel with ordered writes, and hold it with a test that counts the requests a window of n issues makes; the same for the backlog-file due check.",
      "class": "Long commands run silent and serial",
      "home": "scripts/retro.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Long commands report progress throughout",
      "body": "The person, 24 Nov: \"no time limit. but updates?\" and earlier \"scripts and tools that take a long time: can they run parallel, and they always need to give status updates\". No time limit is set or enforced. Every long-running command reports, on the CLI and through the `se` MCP server's stream where the host shows it: its phase, done/total where known, elapsed time, and a stall or failure line when nothing advances; independent reads run in parallel with bounded concurrency and dependent writes keep their order. Asked before in #115, #981 and #986.",

If something stays in the documentation forever as it is, then it needs to be cleaned up by the one-time report. One that's just very big can be acceptable.\" The rule: ephemeral list reports (retro docs, collect output, reports) may exceed the cap; permanent documentation stays under it and is cleaned up. Home: the `large` check of `scripts/hygiene.py` (`MAX_BYTES = 200_000`, `LARGE_ALLOWED`), which refuses this doc at about 370 KB; Its fix is #1812: the `large` check skips `docs/retro-*.md`, every other file stays at 200 KB.
